Figure S2 Undergoes RNA-dependent Phase Separation, Related to Figure 2

(A) LLPS of purified recombinant G3BP1 full-length (FL) protein was assessed in the context of increasing concentrations of the crowding reagent Ficoll and increasing concentrations of protein. LLPS of G3BP1 was dependent on G3BP1 concentration and the concentration of Ficoll, Scale bar, 20 μm.

(B) U2OS cells stably expressing G3BP1-GFP were exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM) and further treated with 0.2% Tween 20 in PBS for 10 min. Cells were imaged in PBS alone or with RNase I (1 U/μl) + RNase A (100 μg/ml) in PBS and imaged for indicated time. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(C)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transiently transfected with FLAG-G3BP1 and exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h). FLAG-G3BP1 was isolated and the RNA-binding affinity of FLAG-G3BP1 was assessed by EMSA. Free and bound RNA fractions were quantified and plotted as a function of relative concentrations of FLAG-G3BP1. Error bars indicate SD.

(D) 5 μM GST-G3BP1 WT was incubated with 50 ng/μl of indicated RNA at room temperature and exposed to UV. Complexes were treated with 4 U/ml RNase I for 3 min and isolated by GST pulldown. RNA was then biotinylated for further detection. Complexes were analyzed by anti-GST immunoblot to detect G3BP1and streptavidin to detect RNA.v(E) LLPS of purified recombinant G3BP1 was assessed in the absence of a crowding reagent, 150 mM NaCl, and the addition of long single-stranded PolyA, PolyG, PolyC, or PolyU.

See also Video S1.

Figure S3. In Vitro Phase Separation of G3BP1 with RNA Strongly Predicts SG Reconstitution in Cells, Related to Figure 3

(A) G3BP2 domains are shown aligned with results of PONDR (Predictor of Natural Disordered Regions) and NCPR (net charge per residue; 5-amino acid window) analyses.

(B) The amino acid sequence of human G3BP2 is shown with major domains marked by highlighted colors. Charged residues are shown in blue (basic; Arg and Lys) and red (acidic; Asp and Glu).

(C) LLPS of G3BP1 deletion constructs in which the NTF2L domain, IDR1/2, or RBD have been removed. LLPS of purified recombinant protein was assessed in the presence of a crowding agent. G3BP1 lacking the NTF2L or RBD domains failed to phase separate, whereas G3BP1 lacking IDR1/2 did phase separate. Scale bars, 50 μm.

(D) Representation of the phase separation results obtained in (C).

(E) G3BP1 ΔIDR3 fails to assemble SGs. We tested two IDR3 deletion mutants, G3BP1 ΔIDR3 (Δ410–466) and G3BP1 ΔIDR3 (Δ428–466). Although G3BP1 ΔIDR3 (Δ410–466) accumulated in cytoplasmic puncta, these puntca formed in the absence of stress, did not contain SG markers, and did not resolve after the removal of stress, suggesting that these were insoluble aggregates, not SGs. Modifying these boundaries to residue 428 to 466 (G3BP1 ΔIDR3 (Δ428–466)) alleviated this aggregation, but G3BP1/2 dKO cells expressing this construct failed to assemble SGs. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(F) Puncta formed by G3BP1 ΔIDR3 (410–466) were analyzed by FRAP and showed poor fluorescence recovery. Error bars indicate SD. ***P < 0.001, ****P < 0.0001, ns; not significant by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparisons test.

(G)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-tagged G3BP1 Δ340–415, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. The majority of cells transfected with G3BP1 Δ340–415 did not form SGs, whereas few cells showed weak rescue of SG formation. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(H) Quantification of result shown in (G). Error bars indicate SD. **P = 0.0021 by unpaired t-test.

Figure S4. Multivalency in RNA Binding Mediates SG Formation, Related to Figure 4

(A)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or indicated GFP-G3BP1 mutants. Twenty-four hours post-transfection, cells were lysed in 1x NuPAGE sample buffer and subjected to SDS-PAGE and immunoblotting for GFP (detection of G3BP1) and GAPDH (loading control).

(B)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or indicated GFP-G3BP1 mutants. Cellular protein-RNA complexes were cross-linked with UV and cells were lysed. Immunoprecipitated RNA cross-linked to G3BP1 was then treated with alkaline phosphatase and biotinylated. Complexes were subjected to SDS-PAGE and immunoblotting for biotin (detection of RNA) and GFP (detection of G3BP1). RNA binding was quantified and normalized to protein signal. Results show quantification from duplicate experiments. Error bars indicate SD. *P = 0.0136 vs. FL by one-way ANOVA, Dunnett’s multiple comparisons test.

(C)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or indicated GFP-G3BP1 mutants and assessed by immunoblot as in (A).

(D)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with G3BP1 constructs in which the RBD was substituted with indicated combinations of ZnF domains. Cells were exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h) and stained for eIF3η. G3BP1 with a single ZnF domain did not support SG formation, but G3BP1 with two or more ZnF domains did support SG formation. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(E) SGs formed by indicated constructs were analyzed by FRAP. SGs assembled with 3 KH or ZnF domains were significantly less dynamic than those assembled with 2 KH or ZnF domains. Error bars indicate SD. ****P < 0.0001 by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparisons test.

(F)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or G3BP1 2xRBD and exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM). SG formation was monitored using live cell imaging beginning when arsenite was added to the medium. Duplication of the G3BP1 RBD dramatically enhanced SG assembly. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(G)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or G3BP1 2xRBD for 24 h. SGs were counted as spontaneous (no arsenite) or arsenite-induced (500 μM, 1 h). Cells were immediately fixed with 4% PFA and representative images were shown for each condition. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(H) Quantification of (G). The percentage of cells with spontaneous or arsenite-induced SGs are shown. Cells with at least 3 granules were counted as a SG-positive cell. *P = 0.0431 by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparisons test. Error bars indicate SD.

(I) The ratio of GFP signal in SGs to the entire cell (GFP enrichment in SGs) from results obtained in (G). The SG enrichment percentage of G3BP1 FL protein and G3BP1 2xRBD protein were quantified by CellProfiler as previously described (Mackenzie et al., 2017). Briefly, the cell boundary and the boundary of SGs were identified by CellProfiler. The ratios of GFP fluorescent signal intensity in the SGs to that in the entire cell were used as indicators for the SG enrichment percentages of these two proteins. At least 100 cells were included in each condition. ****P < 0.0001 by unpaired t-test. Error bars indicate SD.

(J) SGs formed by G3BP1 2xRBD were analyzed by FRAP and showed poor fluorescence recovery. Error bars indicate SD. ****P < 0.0001 by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparisons test.

(K)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or indicated GFP-G3BP1 mutants. 16 to 24 hours post-transfection, cells were lysed in 1x NuPAGE sample buffer and subjected to SDS-PAGE and immunoblotting for GFP (detection of G3BP1) and GAPDH (loading control).

Figure S5. NTF2L Domain-Mediated Dimerization and Interaction with Core SG Components Regulate Assembly of SGs, Related to Figure 5

(A) Size exclusion chromatography (SEC) multi-angle light scattering (MALS) on purified G3BP1 at concentrations of 20, 50, or 100 μM protein. Inset shows a distribution of average molecular weights superimposed on the chromatogram of UV absorbance at 280 nm.

(B) Sedimentation velocity analytical ultracentrifugation (AUC) of G3BP1. The sedimentation velocity profiles (fringe displacement) were fitted to a continuous sedimentation coefficient distribution model. Data were acquired at 1–20 min for high molecular mass species and 30–130 min for low molecular mass species. Table at right shows (1) total concentration of sample (mg/ml); (2) weight-average sedimentation coefficient s20 calculated from the 2D c(s, f/f0) model with percentage protein amount of total protein in parentheses; (3) standard sedimentation coefficient (s20,w -value) in water at 20°C; (4) estimated molar mass calculated from (sw, f/f0,w) from the 2D c(s, f/f0) model, with the theoretical dimer molar mass shown below table; and (5) weight-average frictional ratio (f/f0) calculated from the 2D c(s, f/f0) model.

(C) Two-dimensional size-and-shape distribution analyses of the sedimentation velocity data of G3BP1. The transformed velocity data are shown as contour plots (heat maps) of c(M, f/f0), c(s, f/f0), and c(s,M) with 0 fringes/S (white) to maximum value fringes/S (red), with increasing color temperature indicating higher values. Table below shows (1) total concentration of sample (mg/ml); (2) weight-average sedimentation coefficient sw calculated from the 2D c(s,f/f0) model with percentage protein amount of total protein in parentheses; (3) standard sedimentation coefficient (s20,w -value) in water at 20°C; (4) estimated molar mass calculated from (sw,f/f0,w) from the 2D c(s,f/f0) model, with the theoretical dimer molar mass shown below table; (5) weight-average frictional ratio (f/f0w
) calculated from the 2D c(s,f/f0) model; and (6) Stokes radius (nm).

(D) Indicated recombinant proteins were incubated with the cross-linker bissulfosuccinimidyl suberate (BS3) and then analyzed by SDS-PAGE and Coomassie Blue staining. M, monomer; D, dimer.

(E) Extracts from G3BP1/2 dKO cells transfected with GFP-tagged G3BP1 (G3BP1 FL, G3BP1 ΔNTF2L, or G3BP1 with the NTF2L domain replaced with GST) were cross-linked using BS3 and analyzed by immunoblotting with GFP antibody. M, monomer; D, dimer. Asterisk indicates expected location of dimer formed by G3BP1 ΔNTF2L.

(F) U2OS cells were exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), cross-linked using BS3, and endogenous G3BP1 was analyzed by immunoblot with G3BP1 antibody. M, monomer; D, dimer.

(G) U2OS cells were constructed using CRISPR-Cas9 to express tdTomato-tagged endogenous G3BP1. Cells were then transfected with GFP-tagged G3BP1 constructs as indicated, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), fixed, and imaged. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(H) LLPS of purified recombinant G3BP1 was assessed in the presence of a crowding agent, 150 mM NaCl, and indicated concentrations of D/D solubilizer. Increasing concentrations of the D/D solubilizer disrupted LLPS of FKBPF36M-G3BP1, but not of G3BP1 WT. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(I-J)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with indicated G3BP1 constructs, pre-incubated with 250 nM D/D solubilizer for 30 min, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Cells were imaged and the percentage of cells positive for SGs was quantified. Addition of D/D solubilizer disrupted SG formation in cells expressing FKBPF36M-G3BP1, but not in cells expressing G3BP1 WT. Scale bars, 50 μm. Error bars indicate SD; ****P < 0.0001 vs. G3BP1 by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparisons test.

(K-L)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with G3BP1-WT or FKBPF36M-G3BP1 and exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM). After 30 min of exposure, 250 nM of D/D solubilizer was added and cells were further incubated for 30 min. Images show live-cell imaging prior to and within 2 min after addition of D/D solubilizer. SG dissolution following treatment with D/D solubilizer was assessed by measuring the intensity of the SGs on the frame immediately before and after D/D solubilizer addition. Error bars indicate SD; ***P < 0.001 by one-way ANOVA, Tukey’s multiple comparisons test. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(M) Knockdown of caprin-1 and TIA1 in G3BP1/2 dKO cells. G3BP1/2 dKO cells were co-transfected with GFP-G3BP1 and a pool of siRNA targeting caprin-1 or TIA1. After 48 h, cells were harvested, lysed in RIPA buffer, and expression levels of caprin-1 and TIA1 were analyzed by western blot. Error bars indicate SD; **P = 0.0023 for caprin-1 and **P = 0.0039 for TIA1 by unpaired t-test.

See also Video S2 and Table S2.

Figure S6. The Long Central IDR of G3BP1 Regulates SG Assembly, Dynamics, and Composition; G3BP1 IDR1 Is an Autoinhibitory Element, Related to Figure 6

(A) LLPS of purified recombinant full-length G3BP1 (FL) and IDR1/2 (a 192-amino acid fragment of G3BP1 representing the IDR1/2 segment) was assessed in the presence of a crowding agent and 150 mM NaCl. Purified IDR1/2 failed to phase separate. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(B-C)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells (B) or WT U2OS cells (C) were transfected with GFP-tagged IDR1/2, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Expression of IDR1/2 protein segment did not reconstitute SG assembly in G3BP1/2 dKO cells (B), nor was the IDR1/2 protein segment recruited to SGs in WT cells (C). Scale bar, 20 μm.

(D)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or ΔIDR1/2. Lysates were extracted with RIPA buffer, separated by SDS-PAGE, and immunoblotted for GFP and GAPDH. G3BP1 FL was largely found in the soluble fraction (S), whereas G3BP1 ΔIDR1/2 was largely recovered in the RIPA-insoluble pellet (I). Representative images from 3 replicates are shown. Band intensities were quantified based on 3 replicates. Error bars indicate SEM. ***P = 0.0022 by unpaired t-test.

(E)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 or GFP-G3BP1 ΔIDR1/2. Cells were exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and SG assembly was assessed by formation of G3BP1-positive structures. Error bars indicate SD. ****P < 0.0001 by two-way ANOVA, Sidak’s multiple comparison test.

(F)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were co-transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or GFP-G3BP1 ΔIDR1/2 together with HA-G3BP1 FL. Lysates were extracted with RIPA buffer and incubated with GFP magnetic beads for IP. Washed beads were subjected to SDS-PAGE and blotted with GFP as well as HA antibodies. Both G3BP1 FL and ΔIDR1/2 could form dimers with HA-tagged G3BP1 FL.

(G)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with GFP-G3BP1 FL or indicated GFP-G3BP1 mutants. 16 to 24 hours post-transfection, cells were lysed in 1x NuPAGE sample buffer and subjected to SDS-PAGE and immunoblotting for GFP (detection of G3BP1) and GAPDH (loading control).

(H)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were reconstituted with GFP-tagged G3BP1 (WT, ΔIDR1, or ΔIDR2), exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(I) Amino acid sequence of constructs shown in Figure 6G.

(J-L)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with indicated G3BP1 mutants, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Scale bars, 20 μm.

(M-N)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with either WT or a mutant G3BP1 construct in which the IDR2 was duplicated. Cells were exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h) and stained for eIF3η. Cells were imaged and the percentage of cells positive for SGs was quantified. G3BP1–2xIDR2 supported SG formation more efficiently than G3BP1 WT. Scale bar, 20 μm. Error bars indicate SD. ***P =0.0004 by unpaired t-test.

Figure S7. IDR1 Phosphorylation Tunes Interplay between 3 IDRs and Regulates LLPS, Related to Figure 7

(A) Phosphorylation-dependent charge distribution of G3BP1. Phosphorylation on S149 increases negative charges in IDR1.

(B) Indicated recombinant proteins were incubated with the cross-linker bissulfosuccinimidyl suberate (BS3) and then analyzed by SDS-PAGE and Coomassie Blue staining. M, monomer; D, dimer.

(C) Extracts obtained from G3BP1/2 dKO cells transfected with indicated GFP-tagged G3BP1 constructs were cross-linked using BS3 and analyzed by immunoblotting with GFP antibody. M, monomer; D, dimer.

(D) Ficoll-dependent LLPS of G3BP1 WT and IDR1 mutants (S149E, S149A, or EQ28). Indicated concentrations of purified G3BP1 were mixed with 10% Ficoll in HEPES 50 mM, pH 7.5, 150 mM NaCl, 1 mM DTT, and imaged by DIC. Scale bar, 50 μm.

(E) RNA-dependent LLPS of G3BP1 WT and IDR1 mutants. Indicated concentrations of purified G3BP1 were mixed with increasing concentration of total RNA purified from cells in HEPES 50 mM, pH 7.5, 150 mM NaCl, 1 mM DTT, and imaged by DIC. Scale bars, 50 μm.

(F) RNA-dependent (left) and Ficoll-dependent (right) LLPS of G3BP1RA5 and G3BP1RK5. 200 μM of purified G3BP1 was mixed with either 200 ng/μL total RNA or 10% Ficoll and imaged by DIC. Substitution of five arginines in IDR3 with lysines (RBDRK5) permitted RNA-dependent LLPS in vitro, whereas substitution of the same arginines with alanines did not, illustrating that charge rather than other properties specific to arginine residues is important for LLPS. Scale bars, 10 μm.

(G)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were reconstituted with GFP-tagged G3BP1 (RA5 or RK5), exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(H)
G3BP1/2 dKO cells were reconstituted with GFP-tagged G3BP1 or GFP-tagged synthetic G3BP1, exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM, 1 h), and stained for eIF3η. Scale bar, 20 μm.

(I) The molecular switch in G3BP1 arises from competition between intra- and inter-molecular interactions of three IDRs. Phosphorylation of IDR1 favors the closed conformation by enhancing intramolecular interaction between IDR1 (which is highly acidic) and IDR3 (the basic component of the RBD). Upon increased RNA concentration, RNA displaces IDR1 to bind IDR3, which permits the G3BP1 homodimer to adopt an expanded, open conformation, initiating LLPS.

Video S2. Disrupting Dimerization of FKBPF36M-G3BP1 by D/D Solubilizer Causes Disassembly of Stress Granules in Cells, Related to Figures 5 and S5.

G3BP1/2 dKO cells were transfected with G3BP1-WT or FKBPF36M-G3BP1 and exposed to sodium arsenite (500 μM). After 30 min of exposure, 250 nM of D/D solubilizer was added and cells were further incubated for 30 min. Images show live-cell imaging prior to and within 2 min after addition of D/D solubilizer.

Video S3. FRAP Analysis of GFP-G3BP1 WT, Related to Figure 6.

U2OS cells were constructed using CRISPR-Cas9 to express tdTomato-tagged endogenous G3BP1. Cells were then transfected with exogenous full-length GFP-G3BP1. The relative mobility of endogenous (tdTomato-tagged) G3BP1 was compared to exogenous (GFP-tagged) G3BP1 by FRAP.

Video S4. FRAP Analysis of GFP-G3BP1 ΔIDR1/2, Related to Figure 6.

U2OS cells were constructed using CRISPR-Cas9 to express tdTomato-tagged endogenous G3BP1. Cells were then transfected with exogenous GFP-G3BP1 ΔIDR1/2. The relative mobility of endogenous (tdTomato-tagged) G3BP1 was compared to exogenous (GFP-tagged) G3BP1 by FRAP.
